20:["$","p",null,{"children":"This tutorial simulates the Trotterized time evolution of the tilted-field Ising model with the LTM AI-QEM function.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]
21:["$","p",null,{"children":["Originally conceptualized to understand ferromagnetism and quantum phase transitions in magnetic materials, the Ising model ",["$","$L1d",null,{"href":"#references","children":"[1]"}]," has become a cornerstone of modern many-body physics. At its fundamental level, the model describes a lattice of interacting spins, where each spin can occupy one of two states and interacts with its nearest neighbors. The real-time dynamics of two-dimensional Ising systems become increasingly difficult to simulate classically as the system size and evolution time increase, which makes them an important testbed for quantum simulation. Moreover, because the Hamiltonian is composed of local single-qubit fields and nearest-neighbor two-qubit interactions, its time evolution can be naturally represented with sequences of single- and two-qubit quantum gates. These properties make the model a useful benchmark for studying quantum dynamics on present-day hardware and for evaluating the effectiveness of quantum error mitigation methods."],"className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

LTM AI-QEM reduces the mean absolute error (MAE) to 0.055, compared with 0.249 for ZNE and 0.266 for the unmitigated results. The corresponding root mean squared error (RMSE) values are 0.062 for LTM AI-QEM, 0.270 for ZNE, and 0.285 for the unmitigated results.
